Learn, build, and make an impact with MemMachine.\n\n## 🦛 About the Program\n\nThe MemMachine Student Ambassador Program empowers students around the world to become the voice of MemMachine – The Open-Source Memory Layer for AI Agents on their campuses and in their networks, spreading the message of memory, context, and collaboration in AI.\n\nAmbassadors are part of our Hippo Huddle – a global network of humble builders, curious minds, and creative leaders who share MemMachine’s values of Humility, Impact, People, Partnership, Openness, and Simplicity (HIPPOS).\n\n## 🦛 Our Culture (HIPPOS)\n\nEvery ambassador embodies our values:\n\n- Humility: Learn, grow, and represent with grace. We’re new, curious, and proud to learn from every experience.\n- Impact: Focus on meaningful change — not vanity metrics. Inspire others to use AI to solve real problems.\n- People: Build authentic connections. Host events that make everyone feel seen and welcome.\n- Partnership: Collaborate with peers, clubs, and other ambassadors. Big ideas happen together.\n- Openness: Share, teach, and give feedback freely. We’re open-source in both code and spirit.\n- Simplicity: Make AI accessible to everyone. Whether it’s your first AI project or your tenth, MemMachine should feel easy and fun.\n\n## 🦛 Your Mission as an Ambassador\n\nAs a MemMachine Ambassador, you’ll:\n\n- Be the on-campus representative for MemMachine.\n- Host 1–2 events per semester – demos, hackathons, or workshops (we’ll support you!).\n- Build and showcase projects using MemMachine APIs.\n- Create content (short posts, reels, or blogs) to share your learnings.\n- Collaborate with fellow Hippos around the world on open projects.\n- Provide feedback directly to the MemMachine team to shape our roadmap.\n\n## 🦛 Who This Program Is For\n\n- AI professionals looking to contribute, collaborate, and gain early access to MemMachine tools.\n- Developers or vibe coders building side projects and experimental prototypes.\n- Professionals transitioning into AI – data analysts, software engineers, educators, and creators looking to level up their skills while keeping their day jobs.\n- Community leaders, change makers and educators who teach, organize meetups, or mentor others in tech.\n\nNo matter where you are on your journey, the Hippo Huddle is a place to learn, build, and belong.\n\n## 🦛\n\n...\n\n🦛 What You’ll tools and beta features.\n- Spotlight on MemMachine’s website, blog, and LinkedIn.\n- Top ambassadors get referrals or internship opportunities at MemVerge.\n\n## 🦛 Program Structure\n\n| Stage | FURIOSA\n\nOUR MISSION & TECHNOLOGY\n\n### Join us in making AI computing sustainable for everyone\n\nOur mission is to make AI computing sustainable and so that everyone has accessible to powerful AI. We bring together the world’s best engineers to solve the most challenging computing problems and engineer the most impactful AI computing products. Join us on this ambitious and exciting journey.\n\n...\n\n## OPEN ROLES\n\n### Hardware\n\n4\n\nThe NPU, from architecture to silicon\n\nHardware팀은 AI 추론용 NPU의 Architecture 정의부터 RTL 설계, 칩 구현, 평가까지 칩 개발의 전 과정을 주도하는 조직입니다. 연산 엔진, On-Chip 네트워크(NoC), Chip 간 Interconnect 등 칩의 핵심 요소를 직접 정의하고 설계합니다.\n\n1세대 Vision NPU와 2세대 RNGD의 성공적인 실리콘 양산 및 고객사 데이터센터 환경 검증을 마쳤으며, 이러한 두 차례의 Full-Cycle 개발 경험을 바탕으로 이론적 Architecture가 실제 칩의 결과물로 이어지는 설계 역량을 보유하고 있습니다.\n\n현재 팀의 핵심 목표는 3세대 NPU 개발입니다. Latent MoE, Long-context attention, Linear attention/SSM 기반 Hybrid-Architecture, Multi-token prediction, 초저정밀 연산 등 1~2년 주기로 급변하는 AI 모델의 연산 패턴에 대응하기 위해 칩 구조를 지속적으로 재정의하고 있습니다. 시스템 성능의 기준이 단일 칩에서 랙(Rack) 단위로 확장됨에 따라, 최신 모델 구조를 분석하고 연산 병목(Compute-bound)을 파악하여 실리콘 면적 대비 효율을 극대화할 수 있는 하드웨어 설계를 도출하는 데 집중하고 있습니다.\n\n...\n\nHardware팀은 혁신과 새로운 기술에 열려 있습니다. Verilog 대신 Chisel을 이용한 설계, Cocotb Python을 이용한 검증 Flow, Rust로 자체 개발한 시뮬레이터 등, 개발 용이성과 우수성을 가졌다면 다양한 새로운 시도를 적극적으로 채택하고 활용합니다.\n\n수평적인 조직문화로 팀 전체가 동일한 채널에서 분석하고 토론합니다. 연차에 상관없이 기술적 타당성만 있다면 분석 결과를 바탕으로 설계 방향에 대한 시사점을 제기하고, 실제 결정을 바꿀 수 있는 구조입니다.\n\n...\n\n↓\n\nAI Chip Architect\n\nApply\n\n​\n\nApply\n\nNPU의 핵심 아키텍처와 연산 구조를 정의하고 RTL을 개발합니다.\n\n• 주요 업무:\n\n...\n\nSoftware\n\n...\n\nSoftware Engineer, Compiler (AX Engineer)\n\n...\n\nApply\n\n...\n\n또한 Compiler 개발 과정에서 AI로 개선할 수 있는 문제를 능동적으로 발굴하고, 필요한 도구와 automation을 직접 개발하며 그 효과를 검증합니다.\n\n...\n\nSoftware\n\n...\n\n### Application\n\nSoftware\n\n2\n\nKernels and AI services that prove the chip in real use\n\nApplication Team은 FuriosaAI 가속기에서 실제 AI 모델과 서비스가 동작하도록 커널과 애플리케이션을 개발합니다. FuriosaAI의 전체 스택에서 AI 모델의 요구사항을 커널, 애플리케이션, 서비스로 연결하는 영역을 담당합니다. 모델 실행, 커널, 서비스 API, 배포와 운영을 함께 고려해 가속기의 성능을 실제 사용자가 체감할 수 있는 결과로 만듭니다.\n\nApplication Team의 업무는 커널 개발·최적화와 AI 애플리케이션·서비스 개발로 나뉩니다.\n\n...\n\nSoftware Engineer, Kernel Programming\n\nApply\n\n...\n\nApply\n\n...\n\nSoftware Engineer, AI Application\n\nApply\n\n​\n\n...\n\n• RNGD 위에 RAG 시스템, 에이전틱 시스템, 멀티모달 파이프라인, 강화학습 추론 엔진 등 AI 서비스를 구축합니다. • 모델 실행을 프런트엔드, 백엔드, API, 데이터 흐름, 배포와 운영 환경에 연결하는 풀스택 AI 애플리케이션을 개발합니다. • 실제 시나리오에서 지연 시간, 처리량, 비용, 신뢰성, 사용성을 검증하고 개선합니다.\n\n...\n\n### AI Transformation\n\nSoftware\n\n1\n\nAutonomous agents that solve engineering problems\n\nFuriosa AI 내부의 다양한 엔지니어링 문제들을 자율적으로 푸는 에이전트 시스템을 개발하고 있습니다.\n\nNPU Kernel Optimization, Heuristic Algorithm Discovery 등 소프트웨어 영역에서의 최적화 문제, AI Chip 설계나 검증 등 하드웨어 영역에서의 문제들에 대한 범용적인 자율적 최적화 에이전트 시스템 개발\n\n최적화 에이전트 시스템 스스로의 효율적인 탐색 능력을 개선하기 위한 Recursive Self-Improvement 시스템 개발\n\n...\n\n엔지니어는 고차원적인 문제 정의와 평가 등에 집중하고 AI는 주어진 문제에 대한 빠르고 효율적인 솔루션 탐색을 수행하는 역할을 맡는 형태로 엔지니어링 과정 자체가 변화하고 있다고 생각합니다. 이런 관점에 부합하는 AI 시스템을 만들어 가고 있습니다.\n\n...\n\n↓\n\nSoftware Engineer, Machine Learning Engineer (Agentic AI)\n\nApply\n\n​\n\nApply\n\nMachine Learning Engineer (Agentic AI)는 LLM 기반 AI Agent가 복잡한 문제를 스스로 계획하고 해결할 수 있도록 Agent의 추론, 계획 수립, 도구 활용, 메모리 등 핵심 동작 방식을 연구하고 구현하는 역할입니다. 여러 Agent가 협업하여 문제를 해결하는 Multi-Agent Workflow를 설계하고, Agent가 생성한 결과를 자동으로 실행·검증·평가하는 시스템을 개발합니다. 또한 LLM-as-a-Judge, Benchmark 등 다양한 평가 방법을 통해 Agent의 성능을 분석하고 지속적으로 개선합니다. 나아가 SFT, RL 등 LLM Post-training 기법을 실험하고, 빠르게 발전하는 Agentic AI 연구를 실제 엔지니어링 문제 해결에 적용하는 업무를 수행합니다.\n\n...\n\nPre-training과 post-training을 통해 모델의 성능을 높이고, inference optimization과 agent 연구를 통해 모델이 복잡한 문제를 더 빠르고 안정적으로 해결할 수 있도록 합니다.\n\n...\n\n### Solution Architect\n\n1\n\nBringing Furiosa RNGD from PoC to production with our customers\n\n고객의 요구사항과 기술 과제를 분석해 적합한 솔루션을 제안하고, FuriosaAI NPU 기반의 AI 추론 환경을 구현하고 개선합니다. Furiosa SDK로 추론 프로그램과 데모를 개발하고, PoC와 성능 및 정확도 벤치마크를 수행하며 GPU 등 다른 환경과 비교합니다. 고객, 파트너, 개발자에게 제품의 기술적 특성과 활용 방법을 설명하고, 가이드와 교육을 통해 제품 도입과 활용을 지원합니다. 또한 현장의 요구사항과 피드백을 엔지니어링 팀과 공유하며 제품 개선에 기여합니다.\n\n...\n\n↓\n\nSolution Architect (Junior)\n\nApply\n\n​\n\nApply\n\n고객의 기술 과제를 이해하고, NPU와 Furiosa SDK를 활용해 AI 추론 환경을 설계, 구현, 개선하는 역할입니다.\n고객 요구사항 분석과 솔루션 제안부터 코드 작성, 성능과 정확도 검증까지 참여합니다. 직접 구현하고 실험하며 문제를 해결하고, 기술적 판단의 근거와 검증 결과를 명확히 설명해 고객의 의사결정을 돕습니다.\n주니어와 시니어를 함께 모집하며, 경험과 역량에 따라 담당 범위와 책임 수준을 정합니다. 구체적인 과제는 온보딩 과정에서 개인의 역량과 팀의 목표를 고려해 함께 조율합니다.\n\n## Interview Process\n\n01\n\n### Take-Home Assignment\n\nThis assignment is designed to reflect the type of work you would do in the role. We’ll look at how you approach and solve real-world problems, as well as the quality and completeness of your thought process and answers.\n\n02\n\n### Technical Interview\n\nor Live Coding Interview\n\nThis interview gives us a chance to see how you approach and solve technical problems in real time. We’re interested in your technical knowledge, thought process, and how you communicate your ideas.\n\n03\n\n### Role Specific Interview\n\n(Presentation or Panel Interview)\n\nThis is an opportunity to dive deeper into your past work, projects and technical experience, either through a presentation or a one-on-one discussion. We’ll explore the depth of your technical expertise and how your skills align with the role.\n\n04\n\n### Team Fit Interview\n\nThis is a chance to get to know each other better. We’ll explore how your values and ways of working align with ours, while giving you the opportunity to also decide whether FuriosaAI is the right fit for you.\n\nPlease note that the interview process may vary by role.\n\n## FAQ\n\nIn what format should I submit my application and résumé?\n\nAll applications for roles in Korea are free-format, and you can submit them in either Korean or English.\n\nCan I apply without a master's or PhD degree?\n\nYes. However, this may differ by position, so please check the job posting for detailed qualifications.\n\nCan people who have already graduated apply, not just those about to graduate?\n\nYes, graduates can apply as well.\n\nCan I apply as Technical Research Personnel (alternative military service)?\n\nYes. Placements and transfers of Technical Research Personnel follow the relevant regulations and guidelines of the Military Manpower Administration, and eligibility may vary depending on your military service status and qualifications.\n\nCan I apply to more than one role?\n\nWhen filling out the application, you can choose a first- and second-choice role. Please select the roles you'd like to apply for in order of priority.\n\nCan I apply without attending a campus recruiting event?\n\nYes. These fees are separate from the Vector application and are paid directly to the university. Application fees vary by institution and program, so please check the relevant university graduate program website for the most up-to-date information.\n\n### Does applying through the Program guarantee admission to a graduate program?\n\nNo. Submitting an application does not guarantee contact from a Faculty Member, an interview, a research opportunity, supervision, funding, or admission to a university graduate program. Faculty participation varies year to year depending on research interests, funding availability, and capacity to supervise.\n\n...\n\nWhen you apply to the Vector Institute, you will be asked to select up to five Faculty Members or Faculty Affiliates you are interested in doing research with. Once Faculty have had a chance to review all the applications, they may reach out to you if they believe there is a strong match between your background, research interests, and their research program.\n\n### What is the relationship between the Vector Graduate Program and university applications?\n\nThe program facilitates connections between prospective students and Vector Faculty based on shared research interests. It is not a substitute for the university graduate application process. If a Faculty Member expresses interest to supervise you, you must still apply directly to and be admitted by the relevant university. Admission decisions and funding are made independently by the universities and Faculty Members in accordance with their own policies.\n\n...\n\nYes, international applicants are welcome to apply. ... Expand the ecosystem: Build a rich network of tools that extend Fireworks’ capabilities. 🌱\n2. Showcase interoperability: Demonstrate how Fireworks works with diverse tools to solve real-world challenges. 🌍\n3. Support innovation: Encourage the creation of impactful generative AI solutions. 💡\n4. Promote collaboration: Highlight shared contributions through joint marketing, workshops, and developer resources. 🤝\n\n---\n\n## Types of developer partners\n\n1. Native integrations 🛠️ Tools with direct integration into Fireworks APIs or SDKs, offering seamless plug-and-play functionality. Examples include official connectors, plugins, and platform integrations.\n2. Compatible workflows Tools or platforms that interoperate with Fireworks through shared APIs, workflows, or third-party bridges. Examples include vector stores, training tools, and monitoring solutions that work alongside Fireworks.\n\n---\n\n# What does a developer integration look like?\n\nA developer integration can include:\n\n- Native integrations: Fully integrated tools or connectors offering seamless user experiences.\n- Workflow compatibility: Examples and documentation showing how a tool works with Fireworks APIs.\n- Developer resources: Contributed guides, notebooks, and sample repositories to enable other users.\n\nExamples:\n\n- Native integration: A plugin for a vector database that directly connects with Fireworks’ RAG workflows.\n- Compatible workflow: A step-by-step guide for using Fireworks APIs alongside an MLOps monitoring tool.\n\n---\n\n# How to apply\n\n### Step 1: Demonstrate compatibility or build integration 🔍\n\n- Native integrations: Develop a connector or integration directly into Fireworks APIs or SDKs.\n- Compatible workflows: Validate how your tool works with Fireworks workflows and APIs.\n- Prepare resources such as GitHub repos, notebooks, or workflow guides.\n\n### Step 2: Submit your application 📤\n\n1. Submit your contribution Publish the materials in your own public GitHub repository or supporting documentation. Include those links when contacting Developer Relations.\n2. Contact developer relations\nFor guidance, reach out to DevRel (devrel@fireworks.ai).\n\n### Step 3: Review and feedback ✅\n\n- Fireworks developer relations will review your submission to ensure technical accuracy and alignment with program goals.\n- Once approved, your integration or workflow will be published in Fireworks documentation and promoted through official channels.\n\n---\n\n# Benefits of becoming a Fireworks developer partner 🌟\n\n1. Ecosystem visibility Be featured in Fireworks documentation and resources as a trusted integration. Gain recognition within the growing LLMOps/Gen-Ops developer community.\n2. Community collaboration Join a network of ecosystem partners working to push generative AI innovation forward. Share insights and learn from other projects in the LLMOps/Gen-Ops space.\n\n---\n\n# Program FAQ ❓\n\nQ: Who can apply to the Developer Partners program?\nA: Tools, platforms, and projects that either integrate natively with Fireworks or demonstrate compatibility through workflows are welcome to apply.\n\nQ: What types of contributions are required?\nA: Contributions can include technical documentation, integration guides, sample workflows, GitHub repos, and co-marketing materials.\n\nQ: Is there a cost to participate?\nA: No, the Developer Partners program is free.\n\nQ: Can compatible workflows evolve into native integrations?\nA: Yes! You, as a member of any developer ambassador program, are expected to produce technical content in the form of:\n\n* text (blog posts)\n* audio (podcasts)\n* video (YouTube videos)\n* public speaking (talks at meetups or conferences)\n* Open source contribution to relevant repositories, and\n* helping the community by commenting or answering questions on platforms like StackOverflow\n\n### Benefits of joining a developer ambassador program\n\nThose contributions are unpaid, but you might get other benefits from doing so.     "content": "Title: Community - Relevance AI Documentation\nURL: https://relevanceai.com/docs/community\nPublished: N/A\nAuthor: N/A\nHighlights:\n# Community\n\nJoin the Relevance AI Community on Slack or the companion forum\n\nThe Relevance AI Community is where customers, Partners, and Rellies (Relevance AI team members) connect — ask questions, share what they’re building, and learn from other operators.\n\nNote: \n\nAs of September 2026, we’ve moved the Community onto a new platform. The previous standalone forum is being replaced by a dedicated Slack workspace with a companion forum site, so you can participate from either experience. Membership is not carried over automatically. If you had an account on the previous Community, you still need to join the new one.\n\n## How the Community works Copy Anchor Link\n\nThe Relevance Community is a two platform experience, powered by Tightknit. You can join the Relevance Community via a dedicated Slack Workspace, or a companion forum site hosted at community.relevanceai.com, or both! Activity, including posts and comments, will be synced back and forth either way.\n\nThat means there is one Community with two doors in:\n\n## Slack Workspace\n\nReal-time discussion in a dedicated Relevance Community workspace.\n\n## Companion forum\n\nA web forum at community.relevanceai.com. Read, post, and comment in the browser if you prefer a forum layout, or if Slack isn’t available at work.\n\nA message you post in Slack appears as a thread on the forum. A post you create on the forum appears as a message in the matching Slack channel. Comments on the forum sync as Slack thread replies.\n\nYou can use Slack, the forum, or both. You don’t need to follow both surfaces to stay in the conversation.\n\n...\n\n## Join from Slack Copy Anchor Link\n\nThis is the fastest path if you already use Slack, including alongside a company workspace.\n\nOpen the join link\n\nGo to community.relevanceai.com/join. This is an invitation to the Relevance Community Slack workspace.\n\nAccept the Slack invitation\n\nSign in to Slack, or create a Slack account if you don’t have one. Use your work email when prompted.\n\nSwitch to the Relevance Community workspace\n\nThe Community is its own Slack workspace. In the Slack desktop or web app, use the workspace switcher (the icon in the top left) to open Relevance Community. It will sit next to any other workspaces you already belong to.\n\nIntroduce yourself\n\nPost in #say-hi with your name, company, and how you’re using Relevance AI. Then browse #questions and #announcements.\n\n## Join from the forum Copy Anchor Link\n\nUse this path if you want a web forum rather than Slack, or if you can’t run Slack at work.\n\nOpen the Community site\n\nGo to community.relevanceai.com. This is the Tightknit companion forum for the same Slack workspace.\n\nLog in on the forum\n\nClick ‘Log In’ on the top right hand side of the forum. You can log in with your Relevance AI credentials, or your Slack log in. This is achieved through the following sub-goals:\n\n- To provide a welcoming and guided environment where participants can learn to contribute to open-source software, thus improving their ability to develop software in a collaborative manner.\n- To lower barriers for participation of women & other underrepresented groups in software engineering through targeted outreach. For these groups, open-source software development can be even more intimidating than usual.\n- To promote awareness of the RSE profession amongst participants.\n- To foster external contributions to the open-source projects that REG and the Turing work on.\n\n## What is the process?\n\n- If you are a student and want to participate: For students\n- If you are a member of REG wanting to coach: For https://github.com/alan-turing-institute/AutisticaCitizenScience/blob/master/README.md\nPublished: N/A\nAuthor: N/A\nHighlights:\n# Autistica/Turing Citizen Science\nJoin the chat at https://slackin.openhumans.org/\nJoin our tinyletter mailing list \nAll Contributors\n \n\n*Project management and resource repository for the Autistica/Turing Citizen Science project*\n\n# Welcome\n\n#### Welcome to the Autistica/Turing citizen science community.\n\nThank you for being here! 🎉\n\nThis is the **project management** site for the Autistica/Turing citizen science project. Here you can find resources and tasks which are NOT related to the platform development. \n\n**To go the repository where the code for the citizen science platform is being developed, follow this link to the repository: AutSPACEs.**\n\nWe are a group of autistic people, researchers, developers, parents, designers, experts and volunteers.\nMany of us fit into lots of these groups at once.\n\nTogether, we are building a new online tool to help improve environments for autistic people.\n\nWatch the video\n*This short video introduces the goals of the Citizen Science project.*\n\nOn this site we are working together to plan, design, and build the tool.\nOnce we have built it people will be able to use the tool to share their experiences of navigating environments with each other or with researchers.\nThis will be used to share strategies and provide information which can be used to change environments to make them more well-suited to autistic people and their families.\n\n### You can help\n\nYou can help in lots of ways, for example, by:\n\n* giving us feedback and ideas\n* working with us on an area you are expert in\n* looking through the issues list here and commenting or contributing\n* sharing the work we're doing here with others\n* and much more!\n\nPlease continue reading to find out more, or jump to any of the following sections:\n\n* For information on how to get started and join in, go to get-involved\n* Find out more about autism and navigating environments\n* Find out more about how we work together: participatory science\n* Find out more about our community\n* View suggestions and contributions from the community in community-recommendations\n* See how the project is being run in project-management\n* See who's involved: contributors\n* Re-use anything here under our open source license\n\nWe cannot build this tool without the input of a diverse community of autistic people and their relatives and carers.\nWe also welcome open source developers to join us in building it.\n\nWe believe that the people who will be affected most by research should have opportunities to make decisions, set the direction, and be involved in the research process throughout.\n\nIn addition to building the tool, we will use the experience of co-creating it to write a participatory science framework that other researchers and communities can learn from.\n\n...\n\n## Our community\n\nThis project is a collaboration between Autistica and The Alan Turing Institute.\nAutistica is a UK-based charity whose mission is to help autistic people and their families have long, healthy, happy lives.\nThe Alan Turing Institute is the UK's National Institute for artificial intelligence and data science.\nWe have partnered with Open Humans Foundation, who have helped us adapt their file management system for storing the data the tool will collect.\n\nYou can find out more about all these organisations on their websites:\n\n* Autistica\n* The Alan Turing Institute\n* Open Humans Foundation\n\nAt the heart of the community are autistic participants, families, carers, volunteers and open source developers.\nThis is an open project, which anyone Studying elsewhere? Still apply to express interest!\n\nWhat is your current educational status? * The Campus Lead role is only available to undergraduate students.\n\nWhen are you expected to finish university or college? * The Campus Lead role is only available to students graduating after December 2027.\n\nWhat’s one way you use AI that you think more students should know about? * Please submit a link to a video response of 60 seconds or less. Campus Leads should expect to spend an average of 4–6 hours per week hosting events, participating in training and check-ins, and supporting their campus community. Some weeks will be lighter, while workshops and showcases may require more time.\n\n### Will OpenAI provide workshop materials or do I need to create everything myself?\n\nYou won’t need to create everything from scratch. OpenAI will provide workshop guides, templates, training, and resources to help you plan and run sessions on your campus.\n\nEvery student community is different, so you’ll have room to adapt these materials and shape workshops around what your peers are most excited to learn or build.\n\n### How long will the workshops be?\n\nMost workshops should last one to two hours, though the format may vary depending on the topic and audience. We’ll provide flexible workshop guides that you can adapt to what students on your campus want to learn or build.\n\n### Do I need to start an official club to become a Campus Lead?\n\nNo. You can apply and begin hosting events without establishing an official campus club. We’ll provide resources, programming ideas, funding, and support to help you bring students together from the start. We review every application closely and take care to pair each lead with a complementary co-lead. If you have a friend or classmate who you think complements your skill set and helps reach a different part of campus, we encourage you to each apply on your own and mention one another as part of your application.\n\n### Can I participate in another ambassador program while being a Campus Lead?\n\nCampus Leads cannot participate in another ambassador program while serving in this role. You’re still welcome to participate in campus clubs, student organizations, research groups, internships, and other leadership roles, as long as they don’t create a conflict or prevent you from meeting the program commitment.\n\n### Can postgraduate students apply?\n\nThe Campus Lead role is currently open to undergraduate students who expect to graduate after December 2027. Rather than focusing solely on theory, the workshop was designed to be interactive and practical from start to finish. Participants worked through the Edge AI pipeline using Edge Impulse, taking a project from raw data they collected themselves all the way to deployment on real hardware.\n\nWorkshop result: Sea animal detection with Edge Impulse and the Arduino UNO Q\n\nDuring the session, students:\n\n- Built and labeled their own image datasets using mobile data collection\n- Explored how dataset quality and variation impact model performance\n- Created an Impulse for feature extraction and training\n- Evaluated results and discussed overfitting, underfitting, and tuning hyperparameters\n- Learned the concept of quantization to optimize for resource constraints\n- Discovered how easy it is to deploy the final model to an Arduino UNO Q for on-device inference\n\n...\n\nThe Edge Impulse platform played a critical role in this accessibility. By abstracting low-level implementation details, students were able to focus on understanding the big picture — the end-to-end workflow and constraints — all without needing to write a single line of ML code."

The idea is simple but telling. Rather than relying solely on enterprise sales and cloud deals, Cerebras wants to build a global network of community organizers who will bring its inference platform directly to local developers, researchers, and builders, city by city.\n\n## What Is the Program, Exactly?\n\nThe Ambassador Program is structured around a recurring event format called Cafe Compute, a developer meetup that Cerebras describes as a cafe popup for AI builders. In the last few months, the community has already hosted Cafe Compute events in Berlin, Mexico City, Mumbai, San Francisco, Seattle, and New York City. Cerebras is now opening applications for community leaders who want to bring the format to their own cities.\n\nAmbassadors are expected to do three things:\n\n- Host Cafe Computes: Run the marquee developer meetup in their city, with Cerebras providing the event playbook and covering venue and food costs.\n- Grow the local ecosystem: Become the face of Cerebras in their city and build relationships with AI-native individuals and companies.\n- Surface builder feedback: Share what the community is building, what is blocking them, and what they wish existed, with insights feeding directly into the Cerebras product roadmap.\n\nIn return, ambassadors get compute credits for their own projects, event funding, access to a global network spanning SF, London, Singapore, Tokyo, Mumbai, and beyond, and a direct line to the Cerebras team. Upcoming Cafe Compute events are already on the calendar, including a Cafe Compute at ICML co-hosted with OpenAI, and a Gemma-focused event in NYC co-hosted with Google DeepMind at Datadog HQ.\n\n...\n\n## What This Means If You Build With AI\n\nIf you are a developer, researcher, or ML engineer who has not yet tried Cerebras inference, the ambassador program is a low-friction entry point. Developers can easily access the Cerebras Inference API, which is fully compatible with the OpenAI Chat Completions API, making migration seamless with just a few lines of code. The compute credits that ambassadors receive make it even easier to experiment without a budget conversation.\n\nIf you are in a city without an active AI meetup scene, this is also a real opportunity. Cerebras is offering budget, a playbook, and institutional backing to anyone willing to organize. Applications are open now at cerebras.ai/ambassadors, and the company says it reads every application.\n\n---\n\nTitle: Want to take part in the new Cerebras Ambassador Program? You, as a member of any developer ambassador program, are expected to produce technical content in the form of:\n\n* text (blog posts)\n* audio (podcasts)\n* video (YouTube videos)\n* public speaking (talks at meetups or conferences)\n* Open source contribution to relevant repositories, and\n* helping the community by commenting or answering questions on platforms like StackOverflow\n\n...\n\nGroup Leaders take on the responsibility to grow their group membership, host events, and support local initiatives. * For students only.\n* BrowserStack Champions - A global community of passionate QA experts, dedicated to advancing software quality, continuous learning, and making an impact in testing and development.\n* Claude Campus Program - Lead student-driven AI initiatives with Anthropic support. ... The names are all over the place: ambassadors, champions, experts, heroes, captains, MVPs, student leaders, community builders. Some are for students. Some are for open source contributors. Some are invite only. Some run for a few months and then quietly stop accepting applications.\n\n...\n\nIt lists 96 developer ambassador, expert, champion, campus, and community programs.\n\n...\n\n## What's on the page \n\nThe page groups programs by category, so you can start with the ones closest to your work. Current sections include:\n\n- AI-focused ambassador programs\n- AI, ML, and data science ambassador programs\n- cloud, DevOps, and platform ambassador programs\n- open source and devtool ambassador programs\n- testing, QA, and API ambassador programs\n- database and data infra ambassador programs\n- frontend, design, and app-dev ambassador programs\n- enterprise and ecosystem expert programs\n- campus and student ambassador programs\n- general community leader and creator programs\n- paused programs\n\n...\n\n## Who this is for \n\nThis page is useful if you’re:\n\n- a student looking for campus ambassador programs\n- a developer advocate trying to map the space\n- a technical writer looking for community or content programs\n- a meetup organizer looking for support from devtool companies\n- an open source maintainer looking for recognition programs\n- an AI builder creating demos, posts, agents, or workflows\n- a creator who wants to work more closely with technical products\n\n> You do not need to be \"internet famous\" to apply to many of these. For a lot of programs, the best signal is simpler: you already help people learn, build, or adopt the technology.\n\nThat could mean:\n\n- writing practical tutorials\n- shipping open source projects\n- organizing local meetups\n- answering community questions\n- making technical videos\n- speaking at events\n- building demos\n- creating templates, agents, extensions, or integrations\n\n## How to use it \n\nDon't apply to 20 programs at random. Start with the programs where your existing work already gives you a reason to show up.\n\n1. Pick your lane\n\nStart with the category that matches what you actually do.\n\nIf you build with AI tools, look at programs like Claude Campus, Codex Ambassadors, Cursor, Mistral, Lovable, n8n, and DeepLearning.AI.\n\nIf you’re into cloud and infrastructure, look at AWS Community Builders, AWS Heroes, CNCF Ambassadors, Docker Captains, HashiCorp Ambassadors, Grafana Champions, and related programs.\n\nIf you’re a student, check the campus/student section first.\n\n2. Check your proof of work\n\nMost good programs want evidence that you already contribute to the ecosystem. Before applying, collect a few links:\n\n- your best technical blog post\n- your GitHub profile\n- shipped demos or projects\n- talks, workshops, or meetups\n- social posts or videos that helped people\n- community contributions\n\nYou don’t need everything. But you do need some proof that you’re not just applying for swag.\n\n3. Apply where you have genuine interest\n\nAmbassador programs work best when you already use or care about the product.\n\nIf you don’t like the tool, the program will feel like homework. If you already love the tool, it becomes easier to create useful content, give feedback, and help other developers.\n\n4. Treat it as a relationship, not a badge\n\nThe badge is nice. The real value is the relationship.\n\nGood programs can connect you with:\n\n- product teams\n- DevRel teams\n- other community leaders\n- event opportunities\n- early product feedback loops\n- technical collaborators"

，征集基于星火大模型的垂直创新应用。参赛者可围绕阅读、写作、搜索、聊天、问答等方向，聚焦口语学习、面试招聘、论文写作、学习笔记等一个或多个细分应用场景，完成具有创新性、实用性的应用方案，呈现可演示、可落地、具备商业价值的垂类产品。\n\n01.参赛对象\n\n面向全球高校在校大学生（全日制非成人教育的各类高等院校在校专科生、本科生、硕士研究生、博士研究生，不含在职研究生）。以个人或团队形式参赛均可，每支团队不超过6名学生（含队长），每件作品不超过2名教师指导完成。 允许跨专业、跨学校、跨地域组队，鼓励并支持中外青年学子联合组队。\n\n02.赛事报名\n\n1.作品征集期内，选手须在赛事官网进行注册与报名，签署选手承诺、填写参赛信息、领取星火赛事资源包，并完成团队组建。 2.组委会对团队进行资格审查，如身份作假，将取消参赛资格。 3.往届参赛选手允许继续报名参赛，但不可直接复用往届作品。\n\n03.赛事资源\n\n1.选手填写报名信息时，根据需求自由选择申领任一或多种星火资源（至少须包含1类星火API资源）。 2.选手必须调用星火大模型API的相应功能，使其成为作品的核心组成部分，否则作品视为无效。 3.关于API资源，选手用参赛账号登录开放平台控制台（https://console.xfyun.cn/app/myapp）查看参赛配额，或点击右侧弹窗直接跳转。 4.关于API调用，主办方提供API密钥获取说明及调用说明文档，选手须保管好个人密钥，且严禁在非参赛场景下使用密钥。 5.关于训练资源，选手用参赛账号登录星辰MaaS平台（https://training.xfyun.cn/modelSquare）查看使用，或点击右侧弹窗直接跳转。 6.如需更多赛事资源，可发送邮件至组委会邮箱（AlCompetition@iflytek.com）申请增量，注明队长姓名、学校、作品名称、APPID。\n\n04.其他说明\n\n1.请扫描二维码加入赛事交流群，组委会将在群内提供赛制和技术答疑，最新赛事资讯也将第一时间在群内发布，所有报名选手请务必入群。 2.赛事细则可能根据实际情况动态调整，请选手密切关注本页面更新，并及时查收交流群内的相关通知。 3.本赛事最终解释权归科大讯飞AI开发者大赛组委会所有；赛事奖金均为税前金额，个人所得税由科大讯飞代扣代缴。\n\n...\n\n02.作品内容要求\n\n1. 参赛信息（命名【01-参赛信息】）：点击下载【2025届星火杯大模型应用创新赛参赛信息表】，准确填写参赛信息。 2. 应用方案（命名【02-应用方案】）：项目背景、项目阐述、产品功能、技术方案、运行效果、迭代计划、应用价值、商业模式及团队介绍等。PPT或PDF格式，不超过100MB。 3. 演示材料（命名【03-演示材料】）：产品demo/链接及使用说明、演示视频（内容可脱敏）。 4. 项目代码（命名【04-项目代码】）：应用程序源代码、必要的编译产物和依赖库、程序运行指引文档README等，确保可复现。 5. 其他材料（命名【05-其他材料】）：辅助评审过程的材料，不限于数据集、测试报告、图片等。\n\n03.作品提交要求\n\n1. 队长在各阶段截止日期前提交比赛作品。初赛须于7月22日17:00前完整上传第1-3项必选材料，第4-5项材料上传可视为加分项。复赛晋级团队须于8月26日17:00前完整提交第1-5项材料，其中【01-参赛信息】须与初赛提交保持一致，【02-应用方案】须提交PPT格式。逾期未提交，默认以初赛作品继续参与评审。 2. 各阶段材料均压缩为一个zip文件由队长提交，命名为 【星火杯-院校名称-队长姓名-团队名称-作品名称】。 3. 赛程期间参赛团队均可在不改变作品名称和主要功能的基础上，持续推进作品迭代升级，赛程中最后一次提交的作品为最终参赛作品。 4. 评审期间参赛团队须按照评委会的要求补充提交作品有关材料。所有已提交的作品材料原则上不予退还。\n\n创新水平\n\n项目必须使用星火大模型能力进行大学生垂直应用场景的创新开发，形成具备特色的产品。\n\n技术水平\n\n选手能合理运用星火大模型及其他AI技术，并具备一定深度和广度。\n\n应用程度\n\n项目具备较高的应用性、可操作性，能够在大学生领域或行业内快速落地或已落地。\n\n商业价值\n\n项目具备商业化的潜力和价值，能够满足大学生实际应用需求、社会发展需要。\n\n...\n\n项目开发及作品内容达到较高完成度，其余附件材料也相对完整。\n\n组织单位\n\n主办单位\n\n共青团安徽省委员会\n\n安徽省教育厅\n\n安徽省科学技术厅\n\n安徽省人民政府外事办公室\n\n安徽省学生联合会\n\n---\n\nTitle: The National Key Laboratory of Cognitive Intelligence Holds First iFlytek Spark Camp-University of Science and Technology of China\nURL: https://en.ustc.edu.cn/info/1007/4699.htm\nPublished: 2023-09-20T00:00:00.000Z\nAuthor: N/A\nHighlights:\nFrom August 15 to August 25, the National Key Laboratory of Cognitive Intelligence, a collaborative effort between the University of Science and Technology of China (USTC) and iFlytek Co., Ltd, held the first iFlytek Spark Camp. This event brought together 115 students specializing in artificial intelligence from 24 universities across China, including USTC, Tsinghua University, Peking University and Shanghai Jiaotong University. Nineteen students of USTC participated in the camp. The activity was intended to nurture leading talents in the field of general artificial intelligence and enhance industry-academia-research innovation cooperation.\n\n...\n\nStructured around both theoretical knowledge and practical application in real-world scenarios, the camp featured experts lectures and students’ projects. The lectures covered a wide range of topics, including the technology progress and typical applications of intelligent voice and cognitive intelligence, technology principle of large learning models, evaluation system and prompt design skills. Student found inspiration in their interaction with experts and teachers.\n\n...\n\nAt the same time, the lab designed four tracks for real scenes, namely AI+ education, AI+ office, AI+ assistant, and AI+ interaction. It was an opportunity and a challenge for all students in the camp to fully demonstrate their talents, by collaborating in groups to build systems, design posters, organize the road show, display products, and giving an open reply. At the end, the team led by XIONG Rongkang, a postgraduate of the Big Data College at USTC, won the honor of the 2023 IFLYTEK Spark Camp annual champion.\n\nThey secured this victory by undertaking tasks of the AI+ assistant track. The plug-in platform they designed supports multiple games and users’ interaction, using the large model to solve shortcomings of online games. Their outstanding work received high praise during the review meeting and Prof. HU Guoping awarded them the prize.\n\nThe iFlytek Spark Camp, by bridging practical industry with researchers and talents, represents an innovative attempt of collaboration. Students from all over the country obtained a chance to learn about the latest development of artificial intelligence in China. It is a remarkable and inspiring experience for all participants.\n\n---\n\nTitle: 讯飞生态平台 - 创新生态 加速未来\nURL: http://startups.xfyun.cn/support/germer\nPublished: N/A\nAuthor: 科大讯飞信息科技股份有限公司\nHighlights:\n讯飞生态平台 - 创新生态 加速未来\n\n# 萌芽计划\n\n## 服务内容：\n\n- 课程培训： AI大学课程免费开放。\n- 技术支持：开通技术咨询和服务绿色通道。\n- 免费参展：讯飞年度发布会、1024开发者节、生态沙龙门票免费提供。\n- 品牌露出：提供成果展示平台、媒体推广渠道和讯飞品牌背书。\n- 投资聚合：搭建路演平台，提供与投资人面对面交流机会。\n- 入驻空间：优秀团队可优先入驻讯飞在全国各地的孵化基地，并享受本地的优惠政策和全方位的创业服务。\n\n立即领取（申请条件：创始人或联合创始人为在校大学生或高校毕业不满一年的创业团队）\n\n### 扶持领域\n\n- ##### 智慧城市\n\n社区、养老、购物社会信息服务技术和内容提供商\n- ##### 大数据\n\n金融、征信及其他方向大数据+信息系统\n\n信息&数据安全\n- ##### 机器人/智能硬件\n\n功能型、服务型机器人、车载、智能家居等领域\n- ##### 智慧教育\n\n教学、学习、考试、咨询顾问等\n- ##### 智慧医疗\n\n医疗信息化、临床电子病历、医疗影像及诊疗\n- ##### 车载\n\n车机、系统、自动驾驶及电子通信方向\n\n### 常见问题\n\n- 萌芽计划礼包的有效期？\n\n 有效期为一年，自申请通过之日开始计算。 \n- 优惠券如何领取？\n\n 通过审核的大学生创业团队和初创企业，系统自动发放优惠券，用户可在控制台-财务中心-优惠券查询。 \n- 优惠券的使用规则是什么呢？\n\n 优惠券自发放之日起的一年内有效，不可提现、不可转售，不可转至其他账号使用。优惠券如果到期未使用将被视作自动放弃使用权，在平台大促期间不可使用。如满足两个及以上计划条件享受最高级计划内优惠，我们建议您根据企业自身条件选择最有利的计划。 \n- 优惠券类型？\n\n 针对离线语音合成、离线命令词识别、语音唤醒各2张代金券，分别如下： 一张满8000减3000的代金券； 一张满17500减5000的代金券。 所有代金券申请通过后系统自动发放，代金券不叠加使用，每订单仅可选择一张代金券。 \n\n### 申请流程\n\n- 1.信息填写\n\n准确地填写申请信息，\n\n获得入选的成功率会更高。\n- 2.提交表格\n\n我们会进行约3个\n\n工作日的审核确认。\n- 3.审核反馈\n\n审核反馈将在账户信息\n\n和短信中予以通知。\n- 4.申请成功\n\n申请成功后我们将进行电话确认，\n\n礼包领取可以前往控制台。\n\n恭喜你，已通过审核，相关礼包已发送到你的账号。\n\n如有问题，请联系我们： startup@iflytek.com\n\n恭喜你，已通过审核。\n\n如有问题，请联系我们： startup@iflytek.com\n\n对不起，你的申请未通过审核。\n\n如有问题，请联系我们： startup@iflytek.com\n\n如有问题，请联系我们： startup@iflytek.com"

Developers（华为云学生开发者计划，简称HCSD），是由校园大使为代表的校园开发者组织，致力于连接高校师生和云服务，帮助开发者学习提升，推动高校生态的构建和发展。\n\nHuawei Cloud Student Developers（华为云学生开发者计划，简称HCSD），是由校园大使为代表的校园开发者组织，致力于连接高校师生和云服务，帮助开发者学习提升，推动高校生态的构建和发展。\n\n### 3年成立时间\n\n从2022年开始，HCSD已成立3年\n\n### 200+位校园大使\n\n截止目前HCSD已吸纳200+多位校园大使\n\n### 熟悉掌握一项或多项华为云开放能力（必备）\n\n⑤ 作为高校技术社团的组织者或核心成员，具备带领和指导社团成员围绕华为技术开放能力进行学习、交流和创新实践的经验\n\n⑥ 利用华为技术开放能力在开发者社区/开源社区有一定的成果和技术影响力\n\n⑦ 多次主导组织或参与技术相关活动/大赛，并取得一定成绩\n\n⑧ 具备一定的组织能力，有协助开展校园开发者活动、推广大赛和雇主品牌的经历。\n\n...\n\n## 常见问题\n\nHCSD是一个什么样的组织？\n\nHCSD （华为云学生开发者计划）Huawei Cloud Student Developers是由校园大使为代表的校园开发者组织，致力于连接高校师生和云服务，通过互动交流、学习内容共享、实践项目建设，影响并帮助高校学生在云服务领域进行学习和创新。\n\n成为校园大使需要具备的条件？\n\n1.必须是在校学生，校园达人，有一定的影响力，计算机协会/技术社团负责人优先；\n\n2.计算机/信息技术相关专业；热爱前沿技术和开发，认可华为云开发者生态，勇于探索，敢于创新；\n\n3.执行力MAX，有想法，有创意，社交技能强，善于团队协作，乐于分享；\n\n4.具备团队管理运营能力，包括活动组织、扩大HCSD规模、提升HCSD成员数等；\n\n如何选拔华为云校园大使？\n\n华为云校园大使可以通过两种方式选拔：第一种是由华为合作院校推荐，第二种是由个人自主申请。两种方式提交上来的候选人都需要经过资格初审、专家复审/面试/综合终评最终选拔出华为云校园大使。\n\n审核结果何时以什么方式公布？\n\n个人自主申请审核结果从收到候选人认证资料后，30个工作日内完成审核；华为合作院校推荐的审核结果从收到候选人认证资料后，15个工作日内完成审核。\n\n华为云校园大使有效期多长？\n\n华为云校园大使从获得认证的日期开始，一个自然年为一个认证周期，在此期间，享有华为云校园大使荣誉称号及相应权益；到期后，将根据期间的贡献和成就，重新评估华为云校园大使的资格。\n\n---\n\nTitle: Help students gain confidence using ChatGPT and Codex while building projects together.\n\nStudents have been among the first to find new ways AI can support learning, creativity, and problem-solving. As OpenAI builds more capable tools, we believe students will continue leading the way and bring others along with them.\n\nThe OpenAI Student Collective gives undergraduate Campus Leads the tools, training, funding, and support to help that energy grow on campus.\n\nWe’re looking for students excited to bring peers from every field together, help them gain confidence with OpenAI tools, and showcase what they create with a global community.\n\n### How the program works\n\nCampus Leads work in pairs to create a place where students learn the latest AI tools, start projects, and share what they create along the way.\n\n#### Workshops\n\nWelcoming events where students learn new AI skills, explore what they can make, and choose a project to keep building during Studio Hours.\n\nFour per semester\n\n#### Studio Hours\n\nWeekly coworking sessions where students spend focused time on their projects, connect with peers, and get help when they need it.\n\n#### Showcases\n\nCampus-wide celebrations of what students are creating with AI.\n\nOnce each semester\n\n#### Community\n\nA shared space for students to stay connected, ask questions, and discover upcoming opportunities.\n\nOngoing\n\n### What you’ll receive\n\n- A ChatGPT subscription and Codex credits for the duration of the program\n- Funding, credits, and merch to help you organize campus events\n- Training, resources, and guidance on the latest OpenAI tools and technology\n- Access to the OpenAI team and a global network of Campus Leads\n- A cash stipend each semester in recognition of your time and leadership\n- Opportunities for a HQ visit in June 2027 and other surprises along the way\n\n### From the student community\n\n### How to apply\n\nApplications are open through August 31, 2026 for students in Japan, Korea, the United Kingdom, Germany, and France. Applications for the Fall 2026 cohort have closed in the United States, Canada, and India.\n\nIf applications are closed in your country—or if the program isn't yet available where you study—you can still submit an application to be considered for future cohorts as the program expands.\n\nStudents from every discipline are encouraged to apply. We're looking for undergraduate students who are curious, community-minded, and excited to introduce others to AI.\n\n#### Qualifications\n\n- You love ChatGPT, Codex, and OpenAI tools.\n- You’re 18 years or older.\n- You’re enrolled in an undergraduate college or university program as of August 2026 and expect to graduate after December 2027.\n- You’re committed to 4–6 hours per week of participation from August 2026–June 2027.\n- You study in the United States, Canada, the United Kingdom, France, Germany, India, Japan, or South Korea and have work authorization in your country of study. Studying elsewhere? You can register your interest by submitting an application, and we’ll let you know when the Collective becomes available in your country.\n- You’re not currently an ambassador or intern for any other AI programs or companies.\n\nHow old are you? * Campus Leads must be at least 18 years of age.\n\nWhat country is your university or college located in? * The program is initially available in United States, Canada, the United Kingdom, France, Germany, India, Japan, or South Korea. Studying elsewhere? Still apply to express interest!\n\nWhat is your current educational status? * The Campus Lead role is only available to undergraduate students.\n\nWhen are you expected to finish university or college? * The Campus Lead role is only available to students graduating after December 2027.\n\n...\n\nWe care more that you’re curious, eager to learn, and know how ... We’ll provide training and support to help you build confidence with new tools before introducing them on campus.\n\n...\n\n### What happens after I apply?\n\nAfter reviewing applications, we'll invite selected applicants to interview in August so we can learn more about their motivation, initiative, and community mindset. Accepted Campus Leads will complete onboarding before launching campus programming in the fall.\n\n### Who can I invite to participate in the community on my campus?\n\nCampus Leads will bring together students from across majors, experience levels, and campus communities. Events aren’t only for technical students or those already experimenting with AI—they should also feel welcoming and accessible to students who are just getting started. The goal is to create a space where anyone curious about AI can learn, experiment, and build alongside their peers.\n\n### What is the overall time commitment for a Campus Lead?\n\nThe program runs from onboarding in late August 2026 through June 2027, with campus programming beginning in September. Campus Leads should expect to spend an average of 4–6 hours per week hosting events, participating in training and check-ins, and supporting their campus community.\n\n...\n\n### Do I need to start an official club to become a Campus Lead?\n\nNo. You can apply and begin hosting events without establishing an official campus club. We’ll provide resources, programming ideas, funding, and support to help you bring students together from the start. Program Overview \n\nThe NVIDIA Certified Instructor Program (CIP) enables candidates to become certified to teach NVIDIA workshops. The program connects qualified instructors with high-quality training and hands-on course materials, lab access, and fully configured, GPU-accelerated workstations in the cloud. Through this program, candidates can be certified to teach workshops offered by:\n\n- Deep Learning Institute (DLI): Provides training designed for those who build AI applications, including developers, data scientists, and software engineers.\n- NVIDIA Academy: Provides training designed for those who build, deploy, operate, and maintain AI infrastructure, including system administrators and network engineers.\n\n## General Qualifications \n\nTo qualify for this program, candidates must fit into one of the following categories:\n\n- Associated with an organization that is an NVIDIA Authorized Learning Partner or Education Services Partner\n- Employed by NVIDIA \n- For DLI Workshops only: Currently employed by an academic institution that qualifies for the DLI University Ambassador Program. Candidates in this category must: \n\n- Be a current faculty or a full-time lecturer at an academic institution\n- Teach at least two workshops every 12 months to at least 40 total students.\n\nNote that teaching assistants, postdocs, and students at any level (undergraduate, graduate, or PhD) do not qualify.\n\nIn addition, candidates must meet the technical qualifications listed by workshop or for the selected Learning Path as this is not a train-the-trainer program.\n\nConsideration for acceptance into the program is based on: \n\n- Teaching experience\n- Expertise in Learning Path topics covered in workshops\n- Satisfaction of certification exam prerequisites \n- Agreement to the terms in the Certified Instructor Agreement\n\n## Instructor Certification Process for Developer Workshops \n\nStep 1: Qualifications\n\nStep 2: Apply\n\nStep 3: Get Certified\n\nStep 4: Start Teaching\n\n...\n\n## Apply \n\nUniversity Ambassador applications are by invitation only. Candidates must be nominated by an NVIDIA sponsor.\n\n Authorized Learning Partner applicants and NVIDIANs should reach out to the DLI team directly for intake.\n\n## Get Certified \n\nDLI Instructor Certification features a Learning Path-based framework. Successful candidates will be certified to teach all workshops within their selected DLI Learning Path.\n\nAccepted candidates will be placed into a cohort and given three months to complete the following steps:\n\n- Submit a sample teaching video\n- Complete student assessments for all workshops within the Learning Path\n- Optionally, review recordings of the workshops within the Learning Path\n- Pass the designated Professional-level NVIDIA Certification exam for the Learning Path\n- Pass a 1:1 interview with a DLI Principal Instructor\n\n## Start Teaching \n\nOnce you have become certified, you can now start teaching.\n\nTo schedule a workshop, log into the Certified Instructor Portal and submit the Training Services Request form.\n\n---\n\nTitle: NVIDIA Student Ambassador Program – DIGITAL TWIN CENTER\nURL: https://www.dtc.teu.ac.jp/nvaitc-samp/\nPublished: N/A\nAuthor: N/A\nHighlights:\nNVIDIA’s higher education programs support students with a variety of NVIDIA content, including hands-on workshops, self-paced courses, certifications, webinars, live events, grants, and developer news.\n\n## NVIDIA Student Ambassador Programs in Japan\n\nAmong NVIDIA’s student support programs, the NVIDIA Student Ambassador Program is being rolled out around the world, and the program has been successful in many countries. A violation may not only impact a student's ability to continue with their F-1 studies but may also affect their eligibility for future U.S. immigration-related benefits.\n\nStudents in Cuba, Iran, North Korea, Sudan, Syria, Russia, Belarus and the Region of Crimea are not eligible for the Microsoft Student Ambassadors program. MLX is an Apple deep learning framework similar in spirit to PyTorch, which is optimized for Apple Silicon based hardware.\n\nThis code requires the MLX-specific dependencies from `../requirements.txt` to be installed. We assume that the main requirements.txt is already installed.\n\n## Downloading pre-converted checkpoints\n\nThe pre-converted checkpoints are available at the following URLs.\n\n| Model | Weights | Config |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| 270M | Link | Link |\n| 270M - 4bit | Link | Link |\n| 450M | Link | Link |\n| 450M - 4bit | Link | Link |\n| 1.1B | Link | Link |\n| 1.1B - 4bit | Link | Link |\n| 3B | Link | Link |\n| 3B - 4bit | Link | Link |\n\nNote that these checkpoints do not contain a tokenizer model file, which is required for inference with `inference.py`. Simply place Meta LLaMA2's `tokenizer.model` into the directories to load model using our provided `inference.py`, or if you prefer to use the models directly, use the corresponding tokenizer from Huggingface Transformers.\n\n## Running the model\n\nIn order to run the model, the `inference.py` script is provided. It also provides documentation for how to load and use the model if you are not familiar with language modeling in MLX.\n\nHere's a usage example:\n\n```\nPYTHONPATH=. python3 mlx_examples/open_elm/inference.py \\\n    --model-dir <MLX model directory> \\\n    --prompt \"Once upon a time in a land far away\" \\\n    --max-tokens=1024\n\n```\n\nThis should produce a completion for your prompt.\n\n...\n\nNote that OpenELM 3B should use BFloat16 for both 16-bit and quantized inference. It requires a greater activation range than the other model sizes.\n\n---\n\nTitle: Swift Student Challenge - Apple Developer\nURL: https://developer.apple.com/swift-student-challenge/\nPublished: N/A\nAuthor: Apple Inc.\nHighlights:\nSwift Student Challenge - Apple Developer\n\n# Swift Student Challenge\n\nApple is proud to support and uplift the next generation of developers, creators, and entrepreneurs with the Swift Student Challenge. Android 개발 (Kotlin) iOS 개발 (Swift) CS (자료구조, 알고리즘, 컴퓨터 구조, 네트워크 등) 클라우드 서비스 (AWS, Google Cloud 등) 게임 (Unity) AI (Python, CreateML) UX Researhing UI/UX Design (Figma, Sketch) Product ... 알고리즘 문제 풀이 스터디 CS (자료구조) 스터디 애자일 / 스프린트 방법론 스터디 유저 스터디 / 그로스 해킹 스터디 이 밖에도 소모임 내에서 팀을 이뤄 해커톤 및 공모전에 출전하기도 하고, 개발자 컨퍼런스에 함께 참여하기도 합니다. 또한 두 달에 한 번씩 정기적으로 기획자, 디자이너와 협업하여 사이드 프로젝트를 진행해 앱을 제작합니다. 다양한 분야의 사람들과 커뮤니케이션하고 협업하며 함께 일하고 싶은 개발자가 되는 것이 저희 소모임의 목표입니다.\n\n...\n\n가천대학교 개발 동아리 Team. Take Out \"세상의 창의적인 아이디어를 꺼내다\" 무엇을 하는 동아리인가요? Take Out은 가천대학교 개발 동아리로서, IoT, 자율주행 등 전자 시스템을 설계하고 개발하는 동아리입니다. 교내 중앙 동아리 중 유일하게 자율주행 플랫폼을 보유하고 있으며, 오실로스코프, 3D 라이다 등 전자 시스템 개발에 필요한 다양한 장비를 보유하고 있습니다. 모든 Take Out 부원들은 장비들을 자유롭게 사용할 수 있습니다. 가입 신청은 어떻게 하나요? 매년 신입부원을 모집하고 있습니다. 가입을 원하시는 분들은 가입 신청서 링크를 통해 가입하실 수 있습니다. 가입 조건이 있나요? 가입 조건은 없으나, 회원제로 운영됩니다. 신규회원 : 가입 후 동아리 신입부원 스터디를 듣고 있는 회원 준회원 : 스터디를 들었지만 미니 프로젝트를 하지 않은 회원 정회원 : 미니 프로젝트를 진행했거나, 동아리 이름으로 교외 대회에서 수상을 한 회원 따로 공부하면 좋은게 있나요?\n\n---\n\nTitle: Galaxy Campus 2025 | FAQs | Samsung Philippines\nURL: https://www.samsung.com/ph/galaxy-campus/faqs/\nPublished: N/A\nAuthor: N/A\nHighlights:\n# Galaxy Campus\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions\n\nWe'll answer your questions about Galaxy Campus.\n\n How do I become a Galaxy Campus Student Ambassador? \n\nInterested applicants will be asked to submit a short video introducing themselves and explaining why they're the perfect fit for the Galaxy Campus program.\n\nShortlisted candidates will be invited to a panel interview (either face-to-face or virtual) with Samsung Philippines. [사진=KT]\n\n선발된 Y퓨처리스트는 팀 프로젝트와 KT Y브랜드 마케팅에 참여하며 실무 경험과 역량을 쌓을 기회를 얻는다. 특히 Z세대 트렌드 키워드를 발굴하고, 이를 기반으로 한 마케팅 아이디어가 KT의 상품과 캠페인에 반영되는 등 Z세대와의 소통 창구 역할도 수행한다.\n\n지난해 Y퓨처리스트들은 '독파민', '페르소비', 'AI작' 등 Z세대 트렌드 키워드를 발표했으며, 이는 실제로 다수의 기사에 인용되어 화제가 됐다. 또한, Y퓨처리스트들의 아이디어로 진행된 '삽시도 꿈그리미' 캠페인에서는 숏폼 제작, 벽화 그리기 등 의미 있는 활동이 이뤄졌다.\n\n권희근 KT 마케팅혁신본부장(상무)은 \"Y퓨처리스트의 열정과 노력은 Y브랜드와 KT 마케팅의 다채로운 성장을 이끄는 동력이었다\"며 \"대학생들의 재능이 더 밝게 빛날 수 있도록 적극 지원하겠다\"고 말했다.\n\n---\n\nTitle: pransh15/DeveloperAmbassadorPrograms\nURL: https://github.com/pransh15/DeveloperAmbassadorPrograms\nPublished: N/A\nAuthor: N/A\nHighlights:\n# Developer Ambassador Programs\n\nA curated list of developer ambassador, expert, champion, creator, community-leader, and student ambassador programs.\n\nA list of **96 active developer programs**.
